[codex] +# Dependency Automation
[codex] +
[codex] +**Status:** Local configuration and audit workflow prepared; automation is not
[codex] +installed or active. Authenticated acceptance is pending the system-wide
[codex] +activation phase.
[codex] +
[codex] +This repository uses Renovate as the sole owner of dependency-update pull
[codex] +requests. GitHub's dependency graph and Dependabot alerts provide alert-only
[codex] +vulnerability evidence; Dependabot version-update and security-update pull
[codex] +requests must remain disabled. The shared operating policy is owned by the
[codex] +[orchestration dependency-automation guide](https://github.com/budgetanalyzer/orchestration/blob/main/docs/dependency-automation.md).
[codex] +
[codex] +## Update discovery
[codex] +
[codex] +`renovate.json` extends the Budget Analyzer shared preset. Renovate discovers:
[codex] +
[codex] +- direct npm dependencies and lockfile selections from `package.json` and
[codex] +  `package-lock.json`;
[codex] +- Node and NGINX build/runtime images, tags, and digests in all three
[codex] +  Dockerfiles;
[codex] +- pinned GitHub Actions and `actions/setup-node` runtime inputs in the workflow
[codex] +  files.
[codex] +
[codex] +Routine updates and lockfile maintenance run on the shared weekly schedule.
[codex] +React and React DOM updates are grouped, as are the Vitest runner, UI, and
[codex] +coverage packages. The shared policy disables automerge, keeps patch, minor,
[codex] +and major proposals distinguishable, and requires Dependency Dashboard approval
[codex] +for majors. Toolchain majors such as TypeScript, Vite, Vitest, ESLint, Tailwind,
[codex] +React Router, and Node therefore remain visible for compatibility review rather
[codex] +than being excluded or constrained to historical target versions.
[codex] +
[codex] +Lockfile maintenance is discovery work, not permission to run `npm audit fix` or
[codex] +silently refresh the checked-in lockfile during onboarding. Review dependency
[codex] +and lockfile diffs together. For image updates, retain immutable digests and
[codex] +verify the selected tag/digest supports the required platform before merging.
[codex] +
[codex] +## Audit evidence
[codex] +
[codex] +`.github/workflows/dependency-audit.yml` runs weekly and on manual dispatch. It
[codex] +installs exactly `package-lock.json` with `npm ci`, then independently captures:
[codex] +
[codex] +- `npm audit --json` as `npm-audit-full.json`;
[codex] +- `npm audit --omit=dev --json` as `npm-audit-production.json`.
[codex] +
[codex] +Download the `npm-audit-reports` artifact from the workflow run to compare the
[codex] +raw JSON reports. The artifact is retained for seven days and also includes each
[codex] +command's stderr and `status.txt`. The job summary presents severity counts for
[codex] +the two scopes.
[codex] +
[codex] +An npm audit exit status caused by findings does not fail this scheduled report;
[codex] +the existing backlog remains visible without blocking unrelated pull requests.
[codex] +Missing or malformed JSON, an npm-reported registry error, an unexpected command
[codex] +status, or an `npm ci` failure does fail the workflow. Installation errors remain
[codex] +separate in the named install step's log, while audit errors and partial report
[codex] +files remain available through the always-upload artifact step.
[codex] +
[codex] +The credential-free onboarding run on 2026-09-06 reproduced the saved review
[codex] +against the unchanged lockfile:
[codex] +
[codex] +| Scope                   | Critical | High | Moderate | Low | Total | Top-level vulnerable paths                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
[codex] +| ----------------------- | -------: | ---: | -------: | --: | ----: | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
[codex] +| Full dependency tree    |        3 |   13 |        2 |   2 |    20 | `@babel/core`, `@humanfs/node`, `@vitest/coverage-v8`, `@vitest/ui`, `axios`, `brace-expansion`, `browserslist`, `fflate`, `flatted`, `form-data`, `js-yaml`, `nanoid`, `picomatch`, `postcss`, `postcss-selector-parser`, `react-router`, `react-router-dom`, `vite`, `vitest`, `ws` |
[codex] +| Production dependencies |        0 |    4 |        0 |   0 |     4 | `axios`, `form-data`, `react-router`, `react-router-dom`                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
[codex] +
[codex] +Both commands returned status 1 because findings exist, produced valid
[codex] +vulnerability metadata, and reported no registry error. Compare advisory and
[codex] +package identities in future runs rather than relying on the historical total:
[codex] +new advisories can appear while old ones are remediated.
[codex] +
[codex] +Reachability and runtime applicability remain human triage. In particular,
[codex] +development-server findings are not production-browser findings, and this SPA
[codex] +does not use React Server Components or React Router's server/framework modes.
[codex] +Those distinctions inform priority but do not justify dropping findings from the
[codex] +full report.
[codex] +
[codex] +## Credential-free validation evidence
[codex] +
[codex] +The onboarding extraction ran under Node 24 with Renovate 44.65.5 and found 65
[codex] +dependency occurrences across seven files: 44 npm declarations, five Dockerfile
[codex] +declarations across all three Dockerfiles, and 16 GitHub Actions declarations
[codex] +across all three workflows. The Dockerfile set included the three Node build
[codex] +images, the NGINX runtime image, and the Dockerfile syntax image. The Actions set
[codex] +included pinned action versions and both `actions/setup-node` Node 22 inputs.
[codex] +
[codex] +Public npm lookups produced current-line and later-major proposals, including
[codex] +the saved review's React, React Router, Axios, Vite, Vitest, and PostCSS package
[codex] +identities. Later TypeScript, Vite, Vitest, ESLint, Tailwind, and React Router
[codex] +lines remained visible. React/React DOM and all three direct Vitest packages
[codex] +resolved onto their respective grouped branches, and the enabled lockfile
[codex] +maintenance configuration produced a distinct maintenance branch.
[codex] +
[codex] +GitHub Actions and setup-node lookups reported `github-token-required`; those
[codex] +authenticated lookups are deliberately pending the activation phase. The local
[codex] +Docker Hub lookup did not authenticate and remains an unresolved environment
[codex] +limitation: Renovate repeatedly emitted
[codex] +`ERR_SOCKET_CLOSED_BEFORE_CONNECTION` while obtaining anonymous Docker tokens
[codex] +and recorded `no-result` for `node` and `nginxinc/nginx-unprivileged`. Direct
[codex] +anonymous checks of Docker's token endpoint succeeded, so this is not recorded
[codex] +as an authentication deferral or a successful image lookup. No runtime-image
[codex] +proposal from the local run is accepted as evidence; activation must demonstrate
[codex] +the hosted lookups or stop for diagnosis.
[codex] +
[codex] +## Bot pull request checks
[codex] +
[codex] +Every Renovate pull request remains non-automerged. The existing `Build`
[codex] +pull-request workflow runs locked installation, ESLint, the Vitest coverage gate,
[codex] +the production-smoke CSP gate, and the standard TypeScript/Vite bundle. Reviewers
[codex] +must also inspect release notes, the resolved lockfile change, browser and SSR/RSC
[codex] +applicability, strict-CSP behavior, and Node/image platform compatibility.
[codex] +
[codex] +The [development guide](development.md#repository-commands) owns local build and
[codex] +validation commands. CSP-sensitive dependency changes must also follow the
[codex] +[architecture CSP contract](architecture.md#content-security-policy) and the
[codex] +[external browser harness](testing-guide.md#external-browser-harness); the bot
[codex] +cannot infer runtime stylesheet injection or mobile/browser compatibility from
[codex] +a successful package lookup.
[codex] +
[codex] +## Phase 12 hosted and authenticated evidence handoff
[codex] +
[codex] +No GitHub, registry, or Mend credentials are required or used during local
[codex] +preparation. The activation operator must complete and retain these checks:
[codex] +
[codex] +1. Publish the orchestration preset, install the free Mend Renovate Community
[codex] +   App for this repository, and retain the hosted Renovate log or Dependency
[codex] +   Dashboard URL proving the preset resolves and npm, lockfile-maintenance,
[codex] +   Dockerfile, GitHub Actions, and setup-node inputs are discovered without
[codex] +   authentication, configuration, timeout, or lookup failures. In particular,
[codex] +   prove successful lookups for `node` and `nginxinc/nginx-unprivileged`; if the
[codex] +   local anonymous-token socket failure repeats in the hosted run, stop
[codex] +   activation and diagnose it rather than treating extraction alone as update
[codex] +   coverage.
[codex] +2. Dispatch or observe `.github/workflows/dependency-audit.yml` on trusted
[codex] +   `main`. Retain the successful workflow URL and `npm-audit-reports` artifact
[codex] +   proving `npm ci` succeeded and both raw JSON scopes were captured. A findings
[codex] +   exit status is expected; installation, registry, and malformed-report errors
[codex] +   must still fail the job.
[codex] +3. Confirm the dependency graph and Dependabot alerts are enabled while
[codex] +   Dependabot update pull requests remain disabled. Retain alert URLs and map
[codex] +   advisory/package identities to the two audit reports, including explained
[codex] +   misses and production-versus-development applicability.
[codex] +4. Retain the successful existing `Build` workflow URL for representative bot
[codex] +   pull requests, including lockfile maintenance and a toolchain-major proposal
[codex] +   after dashboard approval.
[codex] +
[codex] +The orchestration coverage report owns final cross-repository acceptance. Local
[codex] +preparation does not activate Renovate, prove hosted audit execution, or establish
[codex] +vulnerability-review parity.

[codex] # AGENTS.md Checkstyle
[codex] 
[codex] **Status:** Active
[codex] **Purpose:** Provide reusable rules for writing `AGENTS.md` files that help
[codex] AI coding agents work safely and accurately without creating documentation
[codex] drift.
[codex] 
[codex] Use this document as a public reference when creating or reviewing an
[codex] `AGENTS.md` file in any repository:
[codex] 
[codex] ```text
[codex] Inspect this repository and generate an AGENTS.md file using the guidelines in
[codex] budgetanalyzer/orchestration/docs/agents-md-checkstyle.md.
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] For remote use, point agents at:
[codex] https://github.com/budgetanalyzer/orchestration/blob/main/docs/agents-md-checkstyle.md
[codex] 
[codex] ## Core Standard
[codex] 
[codex] An `AGENTS.md` file is an operating contract for coding agents. It should teach
[codex] the agent how to work in the repository, where to find current truth, what it
[codex] may change, what it must not change, and how to verify work.
[codex] 
[codex] It should not become a stale inventory of services, classes, endpoints, ports,
[codex] dependency versions, or setup steps that already have a closer source of truth.
[codex] 
[codex] The durable pattern is:
[codex] 
[codex] ```text
[codex] Stable rule + discovery command + source-of-truth pointer
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] Prefer this:
[codex] 
[codex] ````markdown
[codex] Service manifests live under kubernetes/services/. Discover them with:
[codex] 
[codex] ```bash
[codex] find kubernetes/services -maxdepth 2 -type f | sort
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] Exact ports and exposure rules live in docs/architecture/port-reference.md.
[codex] ````
[codex] 
[codex] Avoid this:
[codex] 
[codex] ```markdown
[codex] Services:
[codex] - transaction-service on port 8082
[codex] - currency-service on port 8084
[codex] - permission-service on port 8086
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] The first form survives refactoring. The second form drifts.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Preservation Rule
[codex] 
[codex] When tightening or refactoring an existing `AGENTS.md`, preserve unique
[codex] information. Do not remove a rule, guardrail, workflow, boundary, or operating
[codex] constraint unless one of these is true:
[codex] 
[codex] - The same meaning is preserved in the rewritten `AGENTS.md`.
[codex] - The information is moved to a clearly named, closer source-of-truth document
[codex]   and `AGENTS.md` now points to it.
[codex] - The rule is genuinely obsolete for the current repository, and the rewrite
[codex]   says so intentionally rather than deleting it silently.
[codex] 
[codex] Treat deletion as the last option, not the default cleanup action.
[codex] 
[codex] Do not equate "shorter" with "better." If a section contains non-duplicated
[codex] repo-specific guidance, keep it or re-home it before removing it.
[codex] 
[codex] Example:
[codex] 
[codex] ```markdown
[codex] Keep this kind of direct guardrail if no closer owner doc exists:
[codex] 
[codex] ## Git Safety
[codex] 
[codex] Never run git write commands such as `commit`, `push`, `checkout`, `reset`, or
[codex] branch manipulation unless explicitly requested.
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] If you replace a concrete section with a pointer, verify the pointer actually
[codex] owns the full instruction. Do not replace unique guidance with an empty link.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Consultation Trigger Pattern
[codex] 
[codex] Important pointers should explain when an agent should read the referenced
[codex] file. A path alone says where detail exists; a consultation trigger tells the
[codex] agent when to spend context on that detail.
[codex] 
[codex] The trigger does not need to be a literal `When to consult` block. It can be an
[codex] inline rule, a section-level bullet list, or a short imperative sentence. Use
[codex] the block form when several related triggers need to be grouped together.
[codex] 
[codex] Use this pattern for source-of-truth docs, config files, runbooks, generated
[codex] artifacts, and decision records:
[codex] 
[codex] ```text
[codex] Topic summary:
[codex] - Stable rule or short orientation.
[codex] - Current details live in &lt;path>.
[codex] - Trigger: read &lt;path> when &lt;specific task or failure mode applies>.
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] Good forms:
[codex] 
[codex] ```markdown
[codex] Before writing or modifying Java code, read
[codex] docs/code-quality-standards.md. Do not skip this step.
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] ```markdown
[codex] For comprehensive testing strategies, read docs/testing-patterns.md when
[codex] writing tests.
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] ```markdown
[codex] API routes are resource-based and should stay decoupled from service names.
[codex] Current gateway routes live in nginx/nginx.k8s.conf.
[codex] 
[codex] When to consult nginx/nginx.k8s.conf:
[codex] - Adding or changing a public `/api/...` route.
[codex] - Debugging a request that reaches the SPA instead of a backend.
[codex] - Verifying local and production gateway parity.
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] Avoid this:
[codex] 
[codex] ```markdown
[codex] See nginx/nginx.k8s.conf for routes.
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] Good triggers are action-oriented:
[codex] 
[codex] - Before changing setup, read the getting-started or bootstrap docs.
[codex] - Before changing public APIs, read OpenAPI specs and route ownership docs.
[codex] - Before changing deployment, read manifests, overlays, and deployment runbooks.
[codex] - Before changing architecture or ownership, read the relevant ADRs and active
[codex]   architecture docs.
[codex] - When a verifier fails, read the focused troubleshooting runbook before
[codex]   inventing a workaround.
[codex] 
[codex] Do not add consultation triggers mechanically for every link. Use them for
[codex] references where timing matters or where agents are likely to guess instead of
[codex] loading the owner doc.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Imperative Language
[codex] 
[codex] Write agent instructions as direct operating rules, not passive descriptions.
[codex] Agents follow clear commands better than background prose.
[codex] 
[codex] Prefer imperative forms:
[codex] 
[codex] ```markdown
[codex] Before writing or modifying Java code, read docs/code-quality-standards.md.
[codex] Run `./gradlew test` before finishing Java changes.
[codex] Do not edit generated files directly.
[codex] Stop and report missing prerequisites instead of inventing workarounds.
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] Avoid descriptive forms that leave the action implicit:
[codex] 
[codex] ```markdown
[codex] The code-quality standards document contains Java style guidance.
[codex] Tests are available through Gradle.
[codex] Generated files are usually produced by the build.
[codex] Prerequisites may be required.
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] Use `must`, `never`, `always`, `do not`, `before`, `after`, and `when` where
[codex] the rule is mandatory. Use `prefer` where the guidance is a default with
[codex] legitimate exceptions.
[codex] 
[codex] Do not make every sentence forceful. Repository purpose, architecture summaries,
[codex] and source-of-truth descriptions can be explanatory. Guardrails, workflows,
[codex] validation gates, and consultation triggers should be imperative.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Rule Set
[codex] 
[codex] | ID | Rule | Check |
[codex] | --- | --- | --- |
[codex] | AG001 | Prefer patterns over inventories. | If adding a list of concrete services, classes, endpoints, ports, or versions, ask whether a command or owner doc can reveal it instead. |
[codex] | AG002 | Point to the closest source of truth. | Link to config, manifests, README files, runbooks, OpenAPI specs, package files, or build files instead of copying their exact detail. |
[codex] | AG003 | Include discovery commands. | Give `rg`, `find`, `tree`, package-manager, runtime, or framework commands that reveal current state. |
[codex] | AG004 | Keep stable guardrails directly in `AGENTS.md`. | Include write boundaries, destructive-command policy, security constraints, validation requirements, and workflow rules that agents must always see. |
[codex] | AG005 | Keep volatile details out of `AGENTS.md`. | Do not duplicate full setup flows, generated route maps, dependency tables, class inventories, or API contracts. |
[codex] | AG006 | Make repository authority explicit. | State the repo role, scope, read/write permissions, related repos, and ownership boundaries. |
[codex] | AG007 | Use prerequisite-first workflows. | Tell agents to check required tools, environment, credentials, and repo-owned setup docs before implementing work. |
[codex] | AG008 | Define verification gates. | Name the tests, linters, smoke checks, scripts, or manual checks expected before work is complete. |
[codex] | AG009 | Keep paths portable. | Use relative paths or repo-root-relative paths. Avoid machine-specific absolute paths. |
[codex] | AG010 | Avoid eager file imports unless intended. | Use plain text paths (`docs/file.md`) or markdown links (`[docs/file.md](docs/file.md)`). In Claude-oriented files, avoid `@path` syntax, which pre-loads content at startup. |
[codex] | AG011 | Use hierarchy intentionally. | Put global rules at the repo root and place subtree-specific `AGENTS.md` files near specialized code only when the agent tooling supports hierarchical context. |
[codex] | AG012 | Keep active docs separate from history. | Point to active owner docs for implementation work. Mark archives and decision records as context unless they are intentionally active surfaces. |
[codex] | AG013 | Preserve useful direct context. | Do not shrink `AGENTS.md` into a bare index. Keep enough high-signal rules for agents to act without rediscovering basic operating constraints. |
[codex] | AG014 | Make missing prerequisites explicit. | Tell agents to stop and report missing required tools or environment state rather than inventing workarounds. |
[codex] | AG015 | Explain when to update `AGENTS.md`. | Update it for new instructions, guardrails, workflows, or discovery commands, not for every ordinary code or config change. |
[codex] | AG016 | Add consultation triggers for important references. | For key docs and config pointers, name the concrete tasks or failure modes that should trigger reading them. Inline guidance is fine; a literal `When to consult` block is optional. |
[codex] | AG017 | Use imperative language for agent actions. | Write guardrails, workflows, validation gates, and consultation triggers as direct commands. Avoid passive descriptions when action is required. |
[codex] | AG018 | Re-home unique guidance before removing it. | Do not delete repo-specific instructions just to reduce length. Preserve them in `AGENTS.md`, or move them to a real owner doc and leave a pointer. |
[codex] 
[codex] ## Recommended Structure
[codex] 
[codex] Use the sections that fit the repository. Rename them if local terminology is
[codex] clearer, but keep the same intent.
[codex] 
[codex] ### 1. Repository Position
[codex] 
[codex] State what the repo is and what authority it has.
[codex] 
[codex] Include:
[codex] 
[codex] - Archetype, such as application, library, service, orchestration, tooling, or
[codex]   documentation.
[codex] - Scope, such as one service, one package, a monorepo area, or a whole
[codex]   ecosystem.
[codex] - Relationships to sibling repos, generated artifacts, deployment repos, or
[codex]   shared libraries.
[codex] - Read and write boundaries for agents.
[codex] 
[codex] Example:
[codex] 
[codex] ```markdown
[codex] ## Repository Position
[codex] 
[codex] **Archetype:** service
[codex] **Scope:** payments API
[codex] **Role:** owns payment authorization and capture workflows
[codex] 
[codex] ### Boundaries
[codex] - Read shared contracts from ../service-common.
[codex] - Write application code, tests, and docs in this repo.
[codex] - Do not change deployment manifests in ../orchestration from this context.
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] ### 2. Discovery
[codex] 
[codex] Give commands that reveal current structure and runtime state. Discovery
[codex] commands are better than static inventories because they remain accurate when
[codex] the repository changes.
[codex] 
[codex] Common examples:
[codex] 
[codex] ```bash
[codex] # Repo structure
[codex] find . -maxdepth 2 -type f | sort
[codex] tree -L 2 -I 'node_modules|target|build|dist|.git'
[codex] 
[codex] # Text and symbol search
[codex] rg -n "pattern"
[codex] rg --files
[codex] 
[codex] # Build tool state
[codex] ./gradlew tasks
[codex] npm run
[codex] go test ./...
[codex] 
[codex] # Runtime state
[codex] docker compose ps
[codex] kubectl get pods -A
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] Only include commands that are relevant to the repo and likely to work in a
[codex] normal development environment.
[codex] 
[codex] ### 3. Source Of Truth
[codex] 
[codex] Name canonical docs and files by topic. This lets `AGENTS.md` summarize without
[codex] copying. For high-value references, include consultation triggers so the agent
[codex] knows when to load the owner doc.
[codex] 
[codex] Good source-of-truth pointers include:
[codex] 
[codex] - Setup and onboarding docs.
[codex] - Architecture docs.
[codex] - API specs.
[codex] - Build files and dependency catalogs.
[codex] - Runtime manifests.
[codex] - Script catalogs.
[codex] - Runbooks.
[codex] - Security policy docs.
[codex] - ADRs for decision rationale.
[codex] 
[codex] Example:
[codex] 
[codex] ```markdown
[codex] ## Source Of Truth
[codex] 
[codex] - Local setup: docs/development/getting-started.md
[codex] - API contract: docs/api/openapi.yaml
[codex] - Build and dependency rules: build.gradle.kts and gradle/libs.versions.toml
[codex] - Deployment manifests: ../orchestration/kubernetes/services/payments/
[codex] - Operational runbooks: docs/runbooks/
[codex] 
[codex] Consultation triggers:
[codex] - Setup docs: before changing prerequisites, bootstrap, or local run behavior.
[codex] - API contracts: before adding, removing, or reshaping endpoints.
[codex] - Deployment manifests: before changing probes, ports, resources, or service
[codex]   wiring.
[codex] - Runbooks: when reproducing or debugging an operational failure.
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] ### 4. Operating Rules
[codex] 
[codex] Put stable constraints directly in `AGENTS.md`. Agents should not have to hunt
[codex] for rules that prevent unsafe or invalid changes.
[codex] 
[codex] Common operating rules:
[codex] 
[codex] - Never run destructive git commands unless explicitly requested.
[codex] - Do not commit, push, reset, or rewrite history unless explicitly requested.
[codex] - Do not write outside the allowed repository or owned subtree.
[codex] - Do not edit generated files directly unless the generator is unavailable and
[codex]   the limitation is documented.
[codex] - Do not bypass security, auth, policy, persistence, or validation layers as a
[codex]   durable fix.
[codex] - Treat manual live-environment changes as diagnostics unless they are encoded
[codex]   back into repo-owned config.
[codex] - Stop and report if a required prerequisite is missing.
[codex] 
[codex] Do not demote stable guardrails into a vague summary. If the repository has a
[codex] direct rule like "Never run git write commands unless explicitly requested,"
[codex] keep that exact operational meaning visible unless a closer owner doc now
[codex] authoritatively owns it.
[codex] 
[codex] ### 5. Development Workflow
[codex] 
[codex] Describe the normal path for making changes without duplicating long setup
[codex] docs.
[codex] 
[codex] Include:
[codex] 
[codex] - The prerequisite check or setup doc to read first.
[codex] - The default build, test, and local run entry points.
[codex] - The preferred debugging flow.
[codex] - The correct place to add new files or modules.
[codex] - The docs that must change with code changes.
[codex] 
[codex] Example:
[codex] 
[codex] ````markdown
[codex] ## Development Workflow
[codex] 
[codex] Read docs/development/getting-started.md before changing setup assumptions.
[codex] Use the checked-in scripts rather than reconstructing commands from memory.
[codex] 
[codex] Before finishing service code changes, run:
[codex] 
[codex] ```bash
[codex] ./gradlew test
[codex] ./gradlew check
[codex] ```
[codex] ````
[codex] 
[codex] ### 6. Validation
[codex] 
[codex] Be explicit about completion gates. If validation differs by change type, say
[codex] so.
[codex] 
[codex] diff --git a/.github/workflows/dependency-audit.yml b/.github/workflows/dependency-audit.yml
[codex] new file mode 100644
[codex] index 0000000000000000000000000000000000000000..76b5139b5b89ba9eaa42e10855b5669d75774973
[codex] --- /dev/null
[codex] +++ b/.github/workflows/dependency-audit.yml
[codex] @@ -0,0 +1,137 @@
[codex] +name: Dependency Audit
[codex] +
[codex] +on:
[codex] +  schedule:
[codex] +    - cron: '17 6 * * 1'
[codex] +  workflow_dispatch:
[codex] +
[codex] +permissions:
[codex] +  contents: read
[codex] +
[codex] +concurrency:
[codex] +  group: dependency-audit
[codex] +  cancel-in-progress: false
[codex] +
[codex] +env:
[codex] +  FORCE_JAVASCRIPT_ACTIONS_TO_NODE24: 'true'
[codex] +
[codex] +jobs:
[codex] +  npm-audit:
[codex] +    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
[codex] +
[codex] +    steps:
[codex] +      - name: Checkout code
[codex] +        uses: actions/checkout@v6
[codex] +
[codex] +      - name: Set up Node.js
[codex] +        uses: actions/setup-node@v6
[codex] +        with:
[codex] +          node-version: '22'
[codex] +          cache: 'npm'
[codex] +
[codex] +      - name: Prepare report directory
[codex] +        run: mkdir -p dependency-audit-reports
[codex] +
[codex] +      - name: Install locked dependencies
[codex] +        run: npm ci
[codex] +
[codex] +      - name: Capture full and production audits
[codex] +        shell: bash
[codex] +        run: |
[codex] +          set -u
[codex] +
[codex] +          set +e
[codex] +          npm audit --json \
[codex] +            > dependency-audit-reports/npm-audit-full.json \
[codex] +            2> dependency-audit-reports/npm-audit-full.stderr.log
[codex] +          full_status=$?
[codex] +          npm audit --omit=dev --json \
[codex] +            > dependency-audit-reports/npm-audit-production.json \
[codex] +            2> dependency-audit-reports/npm-audit-production.stderr.log
[codex] +          production_status=$?
[codex] +          set -e
[codex] +
[codex] +          audit_operational_failure=0
[codex] +
[codex] +          classify_audit() {
[codex] +            scope="$1"
[codex] +            report="$2"
[codex] +            command_status="$3"
[codex] +
[codex] +            if [[ ! -s "${report}" ]]; then
[codex] +              echo "${scope} npm audit produced no JSON report." >&2
[codex] +              audit_operational_failure=1
[codex] +              return
[codex] +            fi
[codex] +
[codex] +            if ! jq -e 'type == "object"' "${report}" > /dev/null; then
[codex] +              echo "${scope} npm audit produced invalid JSON." >&2
[codex] +              audit_operational_failure=1
[codex] +              return
[codex] +            fi
[codex] +
[codex] +            if jq -e '.error != null' "${report}" > /dev/null; then
[codex] +              jq -r '"npm audit error: \(.error.code // "unknown"): \(.error.summary // .error.message // "no message")"' "${report}" >&2
[codex] +              audit_operational_failure=1
[codex] +              return
[codex] +            fi
[codex] +
[codex] +            if ! jq -e '.metadata.vulnerabilities.total | numbers' "${report}" > /dev/null; then
[codex] +              echo "${scope} npm audit JSON has no vulnerability metadata." >&2
[codex] +              audit_operational_failure=1
[codex] +              return
[codex] +            fi
[codex] +
[codex] +            total="$(jq -r '.metadata.vulnerabilities.total' "${report}")"
[codex] +            if [[ "${command_status}" -eq 0 && "${total}" -ne 0 ]] \
[codex] +              || [[ "${command_status}" -eq 1 && "${total}" -eq 0 ]] \
[codex] +              || [[ "${command_status}" -ne 0 && "${command_status}" -ne 1 ]]; then
[codex] +              echo "${scope} npm audit returned unexpected status ${command_status} for ${total} findings." >&2
[codex] +              audit_operational_failure=1
[codex] +              return
[codex] +            fi
[codex] +
[codex] +            jq -r --arg scope "${scope}" '
[codex] +              .metadata.vulnerabilities |
[codex] +              "| \($scope) | \(.critical) | \(.high) | \(.moderate) | \(.low) | \(.total) |"
[codex] +            ' "${report}" >> "${GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY}"
[codex] +          }
[codex] +
[codex] +          {
[codex] +            echo '## npm audit results'
[codex] +            echo
[codex] +            echo '| Scope | Critical | High | Moderate | Low | Total |'
[codex] +            echo '| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |'
[codex] +          } >> "${GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY}"
[codex] +
[codex] +          classify_audit \
[codex] +            "Full dependency tree" \
[codex] +            dependency-audit-reports/npm-audit-full.json \
[codex] +            "${full_status}"
[codex] +          classify_audit \
[codex] +            "Production dependencies" \
[codex] +            dependency-audit-reports/npm-audit-production.json \
[codex] +            "${production_status}"
[codex] +
[codex] +          {
[codex] +            printf 'full_status=%s\n' "${full_status}"
[codex] +            printf 'production_status=%s\n' "${production_status}"
[codex] +            printf 'operational_failure=%s\n' "${audit_operational_failure}"
[codex] +          } > dependency-audit-reports/status.txt
[codex] +
[codex] +          if [[ "${audit_operational_failure}" -ne 0 ]]; then
[codex] +            exit 1
[codex] +          fi
[codex] +
[codex] +          if [[ "${full_status}" -eq 1 || "${production_status}" -eq 1 ]]; then
[codex] +            echo 'Vulnerability findings were retained as evidence and do not fail this scheduled report.' >> "${GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY}"
[codex] +          fi
[codex] +
[codex] +      - name: Upload audit reports
[codex] +        if: ${{ always() }}
[codex] +        uses: actions/upload-artifact@v7
[codex] +        with:
[codex] +          name: npm-audit-reports
[codex] +          path: dependency-audit-reports/
[codex] +          if-no-files-found: warn
[codex] +          retention-days: 7

[codex] +# Dependency Automation
[codex] +
[codex] +**Status:** Local configuration and audit workflow prepared; automation is not
[codex] +installed or active. Authenticated acceptance is pending the system-wide
[codex] +activation phase.
[codex] +
[codex] +This repository uses Renovate as the sole owner of dependency-update pull
[codex] +requests. GitHub's dependency graph and Dependabot alerts provide alert-only
[codex] +vulnerability evidence; Dependabot version-update and security-update pull
[codex] +requests must remain disabled. The shared operating policy is owned by the
[codex] +[orchestration dependency-automation guide](https://github.com/budgetanalyzer/orchestration/blob/main/docs/dependency-automation.md).
[codex] +
[codex] +## Update discovery
[codex] +
[codex] +`renovate.json` extends the Budget Analyzer shared preset. Renovate discovers:
[codex] +
[codex] +- direct npm dependencies and lockfile selections from `package.json` and
[codex] +  `package-lock.json`;
[codex] +- Node and NGINX build/runtime images, tags, and digests in all three
[codex] +  Dockerfiles;
[codex] +- pinned GitHub Actions and `actions/setup-node` runtime inputs in the workflow
[codex] +  files.
[codex] +
[codex] +Routine updates and lockfile maintenance run on the shared weekly schedule.
[codex] +React and React DOM updates are grouped, as are the Vitest runner, UI, and
[codex] +coverage packages. The shared policy disables automerge, keeps patch, minor,
[codex] +and major proposals distinguishable, and requires Dependency Dashboard approval
[codex] +for majors. Toolchain majors such as TypeScript, Vite, Vitest, ESLint, Tailwind,
[codex] +React Router, and Node therefore remain visible for compatibility review rather
[codex] +than being excluded or constrained to historical target versions.
[codex] +
[codex] +Lockfile maintenance is discovery work, not permission to run `npm audit fix` or
[codex] +silently refresh the checked-in lockfile during onboarding. Review dependency
[codex] +and lockfile diffs together. For image updates, retain immutable digests and
[codex] +verify the selected tag/digest supports the required platform before merging.
[codex] +
[codex] +## Audit evidence
[codex] +
[codex] +`.github/workflows/dependency-audit.yml` runs weekly and on manual dispatch. It
[codex] +installs exactly `package-lock.json` with `npm ci`, then independently captures:
[codex] +
[codex] +- `npm audit --json` as `npm-audit-full.json`;
[codex] +- `npm audit --omit=dev --json` as `npm-audit-production.json`.
[codex] +
[codex] +Download the `npm-audit-reports` artifact from the workflow run to compare the
[codex] +raw JSON reports. The artifact is retained for seven days and also includes each
[codex] +command's stderr and `status.txt`. The job summary presents severity counts for
[codex] +the two scopes.
[codex] +
[codex] +An npm audit exit status caused by findings does not fail this scheduled report;
[codex] +the existing backlog remains visible without blocking unrelated pull requests.
[codex] +Missing or malformed JSON, an npm-reported registry error, an unexpected command
[codex] +status, or an `npm ci` failure does fail the workflow. Installation errors remain
[codex] +separate in the named install step's log, while audit errors and partial report
[codex] +files remain available through the always-upload artifact step.
[codex] +
[codex] +The credential-free onboarding run on 2026-09-06 reproduced the saved review
[codex] +against the unchanged lockfile:
[codex] +
[codex] +| Scope                   | Critical | High | Moderate | Low | Total | Top-level vulnerable paths                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
[codex] +| ----------------------- | -------: | ---: | -------: | --: | ----: | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
[codex] +| Full dependency tree    |        3 |   13 |        2 |   2 |    20 | `@babel/core`, `@humanfs/node`, `@vitest/coverage-v8`, `@vitest/ui`, `axios`, `brace-expansion`, `browserslist`, `fflate`, `flatted`, `form-data`, `js-yaml`, `nanoid`, `picomatch`, `postcss`, `postcss-selector-parser`, `react-router`, `react-router-dom`, `vite`, `vitest`, `ws` |
[codex] +| Production dependencies |        0 |    4 |        0 |   0 |     4 | `axios`, `form-data`, `react-router`, `react-router-dom`                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
[codex] +
[codex] +Both commands returned status 1 because findings exist, produced valid
[codex] +vulnerability metadata, and reported no registry error. Compare advisory and
[codex] +package identities in future runs rather than relying on the historical total:
[codex] +new advisories can appear while old ones are remediated.
[codex] +
[codex] +Reachability and runtime applicability remain human triage. In particular,
[codex] +development-server findings are not production-browser findings, and this SPA
[codex] +does not use React Server Components or React Router's server/framework modes.
[codex] +Those distinctions inform priority but do not justify dropping findings from the
[codex] +full report.
[codex] +
[codex] +## Credential-free validation evidence
[codex] +
[codex] +The onboarding extraction ran under Node 24 with Renovate 44.65.5 and found 65
[codex] +dependency occurrences across seven files: 44 npm declarations, five Dockerfile
[codex] +declarations across all three Dockerfiles, and 16 GitHub Actions declarations
[codex] +across all three workflows. The Dockerfile set included the three Node build
[codex] +images, the NGINX runtime image, and the Dockerfile syntax image. The Actions set
[codex] +included pinned action versions and both `actions/setup-node` Node 22 inputs.
[codex] +
[codex] +Public npm lookups produced current-line and later-major proposals, including
[codex] +the saved review's React, React Router, Axios, Vite, Vitest, and PostCSS package
[codex] +identities. Later TypeScript, Vite, Vitest, ESLint, Tailwind, and React Router
[codex] +lines remained visible. React/React DOM and all three direct Vitest packages
[codex] +resolved onto their respective grouped branches, and the enabled lockfile
[codex] +maintenance configuration produced a distinct maintenance branch.
[codex] +
[codex] +GitHub Actions and setup-node lookups reported `github-token-required`; those
[codex] +authenticated lookups are deliberately pending the activation phase. The local
[codex] +Docker Hub lookup did not authenticate and remains an unresolved environment
[codex] +limitation: Renovate repeatedly emitted
[codex] +`ERR_SOCKET_CLOSED_BEFORE_CONNECTION` while obtaining anonymous Docker tokens
[codex] +and recorded `no-result` for `node` and `nginxinc/nginx-unprivileged`. Direct
[codex] +anonymous checks of Docker's token endpoint succeeded, so this is not recorded
[codex] +as an authentication deferral or a successful image lookup. No runtime-image
[codex] +proposal from the local run is accepted as evidence; activation must demonstrate
[codex] +the hosted lookups or stop for diagnosis.
[codex] +
[codex] +## Bot pull request checks
[codex] +
[codex] +Every Renovate pull request remains non-automerged. The existing `Build`
[codex] +pull-request workflow runs locked installation, ESLint, the Vitest coverage gate,
[codex] +the production-smoke CSP gate, and the standard TypeScript/Vite bundle. Reviewers
[codex] +must also inspect release notes, the resolved lockfile change, browser and SSR/RSC
[codex] +applicability, strict-CSP behavior, and Node/image platform compatibility.
[codex] +
[codex] +The [development guide](development.md#repository-commands) owns local build and
[codex] +validation commands. CSP-sensitive dependency changes must also follow the
[codex] +[architecture CSP contract](architecture.md#content-security-policy) and the
[codex] +[external browser harness](testing-guide.md#external-browser-harness); the bot
[codex] +cannot infer runtime stylesheet injection or mobile/browser compatibility from
[codex] +a successful package lookup.
[codex] +
[codex] +## Phase 12 hosted and authenticated evidence handoff
[codex] +
[codex] +No GitHub, registry, or Mend credentials are required or used during local
[codex] +preparation. The activation operator must complete and retain these checks:
[codex] +
[codex] +1. Publish the orchestration preset, install the free Mend Renovate Community
[codex] +   App for this repository, and retain the hosted Renovate log or Dependency
[codex] +   Dashboard URL proving the preset resolves and npm, lockfile-maintenance,
[codex] +   Dockerfile, GitHub Actions, and setup-node inputs are discovered without
[codex] +   authentication, configuration, timeout, or lookup failures. In particular,
[codex] +   prove successful lookups for `node` and `nginxinc/nginx-unprivileged`; if the
[codex] +   local anonymous-token socket failure repeats in the hosted run, stop
[codex] +   activation and diagnose it rather than treating extraction alone as update
[codex] +   coverage.
[codex] +2. Dispatch or observe `.github/workflows/dependency-audit.yml` on trusted
[codex] +   `main`. Retain the successful workflow URL and `npm-audit-reports` artifact
[codex] +   proving `npm ci` succeeded and both raw JSON scopes were captured. A findings
[codex] +   exit status is expected; installation, registry, and malformed-report errors
[codex] +   must still fail the job.
[codex] +3. Confirm the dependency graph and Dependabot alerts are enabled while
[codex] +   Dependabot update pull requests remain disabled. Retain alert URLs and map
[codex] +   advisory/package identities to the two audit reports, including explained
[codex] +   misses and production-versus-development applicability.
[codex] +4. Retain the successful existing `Build` workflow URL for representative bot
[codex] +   pull requests, including lockfile maintenance and a toolchain-major proposal
[codex] +   after dashboard approval.
[codex] +
[codex] +The orchestration coverage report owns final cross-repository acceptance. Local
[codex] +preparation does not activate Renovate, prove hosted audit execution, or establish
[codex] +vulnerability-review parity.

[codex] 
[codex] Example:
[codex] 
[codex] ```markdown
[codex] ## Validation
[codex] 
[codex] - Shell scripts: `bash -n &lt;script>` and `shellcheck &lt;script>`.
[codex] - Java changes: `./gradlew test` and `./gradlew check`.
[codex] - Frontend changes: `npm test` and `npm run lint`.
[codex] - Kubernetes changes: `kubectl apply --dry-run=server -f &lt;file>` when a
[codex]   cluster is available.
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] Also say what to do when a gate cannot run:
[codex] 
[codex] ```markdown
[codex] If a required verifier cannot run because a tool, cluster, credential, or
[codex] service is unavailable, report that explicitly. Do not claim the work is fully
[codex] verified.
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] ### 7. Documentation Maintenance
[codex] 
[codex] Tell agents when to update docs and which docs own recurring topics.
[codex] 
[codex] Recommended rules:
[codex] 
[codex] - Update `AGENTS.md` when repo instructions, guardrails, discovery commands, or
[codex]   workflows change.
[codex] - Update `README.md` when setup, usage, public purpose, or human onboarding
[codex]   changes.
[codex] - Update `docs/` when architecture, operations, APIs, behavior, or design
[codex]   rationale changes.
[codex] - Do not update archived docs unless explicitly requested.
[codex] - Do not duplicate a detailed topic across multiple docs. Choose or update the
[codex]   owner doc and link to it.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Specificity Guidance
[codex] 
[codex] Use this table to decide what belongs directly in `AGENTS.md`.
[codex] 
[codex] | Content | Put In `AGENTS.md`? | Better Owner |
[codex] | --- | --- | --- |
[codex] | Repo role and scope | Yes | `AGENTS.md` |
[codex] | Agent write boundaries | Yes | `AGENTS.md` |
[codex] | Destructive command policy | Yes | `AGENTS.md` |
[codex] | Security or compliance guardrails | Yes | `AGENTS.md`, with links to detailed policy docs |
[codex] | Discovery commands | Yes | `AGENTS.md` |
[codex] | Full setup sequence | Usually no | Getting-started docs or setup scripts |
[codex] | Complete port table | No | Architecture docs, manifests, compose files |
[codex] | API endpoint inventory | No | OpenAPI specs, route files, controller discovery |
[codex] | Dependency versions | No | Build files, lockfiles, version catalogs |
[codex] | Class names and package inventories | No | Source code discovery |
[codex] | Historical rationale | Brief pointer only | ADRs or decision records |
[codex] | Generated output definitions | No | Generator docs or generated artifacts |
[codex] 
[codex] Rule of thumb:
[codex] 
[codex] ```text
[codex] If it changes during normal implementation, point to the source of truth.
[codex] If it constrains every agent action, keep it directly in AGENTS.md.
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] ## Anti-Patterns
[codex] 
[codex] Avoid these patterns during authoring and review.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Static Inventories
[codex] 
[codex] Static lists of services, endpoints, ports, packages, classes, or versions tend
[codex] to drift. Replace them with discovery commands and owner-doc links.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Duplicated Procedures
[codex] 
[codex] If a setup flow or operational runbook already exists, `AGENTS.md` should link
[codex] to it and summarize when to use it. It should not maintain a second copy.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Vague Guardrails
[codex] 
[codex] Rules like "be careful with deployment" are too weak. Name the exact forbidden
[codex] or required behavior.
[codex] 
[codex] Prefer:
[codex] 
[codex] ```markdown
[codex] Do not use `--address 0.0.0.0` for observability port-forwards.
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] Avoid:
[codex] 
[codex] ```markdown
[codex] Be careful exposing observability.
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] ### Context Preload By Accident
[codex] 
[codex] Some agent tools support syntax that imports file contents into the startup
[codex] context. For example, in Claude-oriented context files, `@docs/file.md` imports
[codex] the file rather than acting as a lazy pointer.
[codex] 
[codex] For portable `AGENTS.md` files, prefer plain text paths or markdown links:
[codex] 
[codex] ```markdown
[codex] Read docs/architecture/system-overview.md when changing service boundaries.
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] Use eager imports only when you intentionally want the full referenced content
[codex] loaded every time.
[codex] 
[codex] ### Token Minimization As The Only Goal
[codex] 
[codex] Short files are not automatically good. A useful `AGENTS.md` should be concise,
[codex] but it must still include high-signal operating constraints that agents need
[codex] before making changes.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Generation Workflow
[codex] 
[codex] When asking an agent to create a new `AGENTS.md`, require this workflow.
[codex] 
[codex] 1. Inspect the repository structure with `find`, `tree`, or equivalent
[codex]    commands.
[codex] 2. Read the primary human docs, such as `README.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, setup
[codex]    docs, architecture docs, and script catalogs.
[codex] 3. Inspect build files, package files, manifests, and test configuration to
[codex]    discover current commands.
[codex] 4. Identify the repository archetype, ownership boundaries, and closest source
[codex]    of truth for recurring topics.
[codex] 5. Draft `AGENTS.md` using stable rules, discovery commands, and pointers
[codex]    instead of copied inventories.
[codex] 6. Add consultation triggers for references that agents should not ignore
[codex]    during related changes.
[codex] 7. Compare the draft against the existing `AGENTS.md` and explicitly preserve,
[codex]    re-home, or intentionally retire each repo-specific rule that is not
[codex]    duplicated elsewhere.
[codex] 8. Verify every included command is syntactically plausible and every referenced
[codex]    file exists.
[codex] 9. Update documentation indexes or ownership maps if the repository has them.
[codex] 
[codex] Do not generate `AGENTS.md` from assumptions alone. The file should be grounded
[codex] in discovered repository facts.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Review Checklist
[codex] 
[codex] Use this checklist before accepting a new or modified `AGENTS.md`.
[codex] 
[codex] - The repo role, scope, and agent write boundaries are explicit.
[codex] - The file contains discovery commands for structure, source search, and key
[codex]   runtime or build state.
[codex] - The file points to owner docs for setup, architecture, APIs, operations, and
[codex]   deployment instead of copying their full detail.
[codex] - Important references include inline or block-form triggers for the tasks or
[codex]   failures that require reading them.
[codex] - Guardrails, workflow steps, and validation gates use imperative language.
[codex] - Stable safety rules are directly visible.
[codex] - Unique repo-specific guidance from the previous `AGENTS.md` was preserved,
[codex]   re-homed to a real owner doc, or intentionally retired with justification.
[codex] - Validation gates are clear by change type.
[codex] - Missing-prerequisite behavior is explicit.
[codex] - No machine-specific absolute paths are required.
[codex] - No stale-looking inventories of services, ports, endpoints, classes, or
[codex]   dependency versions are maintained.
[codex] - Historical docs and ADRs are treated as context unless explicitly active.
[codex] - The file is long enough to be useful but not bloated with details owned
[codex]   elsewhere.
[codex] - References use plain paths or markdown links unless eager import semantics are
[codex]   intentional.
[codex] - No section was removed solely because it looked verbose. Information loss was
[codex]   checked explicitly.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Minimal Template
[codex] 
[codex] Use this as a starting point, then tailor it to the repository.
[codex] 
[codex] ````markdown
[codex] # &lt;Project> Agent Instructions
[codex] 
[codex] ## Repository Position
[codex] 
[codex] **Archetype:** &lt;application | service | library | orchestration | tooling>
[codex] **Scope:** &lt;owned system area>
[codex] **Role:** &lt;what this repo owns>
[codex] 
[codex] ### Boundaries
[codex] - Read: &lt;allowed external paths or systems>
[codex] - Write: &lt;owned paths>
[codex] - Do not write: &lt;forbidden paths or generated surfaces>
[codex] 
[codex] ## Discovery
[codex] 
[codex] ```bash
[codex] # Repo structure
[codex] find . -maxdepth 2 -type f | sort
[codex] 
[codex] # Source search
[codex] rg -n "&lt;important pattern>"
[codex] 
[codex] # Available scripts or build tasks
[codex] &lt;repo-specific command>
[codex] ```
[codex] 
[codex] ## Source Of Truth
[codex] 
[codex] - Setup: &lt;path>
[codex] - Architecture: &lt;path>
[codex] - API contracts: &lt;path>
[codex] - Build and dependencies: &lt;path>
[codex] - Operations: &lt;path>
[codex] 
[codex] Consultation triggers:
[codex] - &lt;path>: &lt;trigger for reading this owner doc>.
[codex] - &lt;path>: &lt;trigger for reading this owner doc>.
[codex] - &lt;path>: &lt;trigger for reading this owner doc>.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Operating Rules
[codex] 
[codex] - Do not &lt;forbidden action>.
[codex] - Always &lt;required action>.
[codex] - Before &lt;task>, read &lt;path>.
[codex] - Stop and report &lt;missing prerequisite or boundary issue>.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Development Workflow
[codex] 
[codex] - Check prerequisites in &lt;path> before implementation.
[codex] - Use &lt;command> for the normal local run path.
[codex] - Follow &lt;path> when adding new modules, routes, or services.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Validation
[codex] 
[codex] - &lt;change type>: &lt;command>
[codex] - &lt;change type>: &lt;command>
[codex] 
[codex] If a required verifier cannot run, report the missing prerequisite or failure
[codex] instead of claiming full verification.
[codex] 
[codex] ## Documentation Maintenance
[codex] 
[codex] - Update `AGENTS.md` when agent instructions or guardrails change.
[codex] - Update `README.md` when human setup or usage changes.
[codex] - Update `docs/` when architecture, behavior, operations, or APIs change.
[codex] - Keep detailed recurring topics in one owner doc and link to it here.
[codex] ````
[codex] 
[codex] ## Relationship To Decision Records
[codex] 
[codex] This checkstyle is distilled from the pattern-based documentation principles in
[codex] docs/decisions/003-pattern-based-claude-md.md. That decision record remains
[codex] historical context and rationale. This file is the active reusable authoring
[codex] standard for new `AGENTS.md` files.
[codex] 
[codex] diff --git a/.github/workflows/dependency-audit.yml b/.github/workflows/dependency-audit.yml
[codex] new file mode 100644
[codex] index 0000000000000000000000000000000000000000..76b5139b5b89ba9eaa42e10855b5669d75774973
[codex] --- /dev/null
[codex] +++ b/.github/workflows/dependency-audit.yml
[codex] @@ -0,0 +1,137 @@
[codex] +name: Dependency Audit
[codex] +
[codex] +on:
[codex] +  schedule:
[codex] +    - cron: '17 6 * * 1'
[codex] +  workflow_dispatch:
[codex] +
[codex] +permissions:
[codex] +  contents: read
[codex] +
[codex] +concurrency:
[codex] +  group: dependency-audit
[codex] +  cancel-in-progress: false
[codex] +
[codex] +env:
[codex] +  FORCE_JAVASCRIPT_ACTIONS_TO_NODE24: 'true'
[codex] +
[codex] +jobs:
[codex] +  npm-audit:
[codex] +    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
[codex] +
[codex] +    steps:
[codex] +      - name: Checkout code
[codex] +        uses: actions/checkout@v6
[codex] +
[codex] +      - name: Set up Node.js
[codex] +        uses: actions/setup-node@v6
[codex] +        with:
[codex] +          node-version: '22'
[codex] +          cache: 'npm'
[codex] +
[codex] +      - name: Prepare report directory
[codex] +        run: mkdir -p dependency-audit-reports
[codex] +
[codex] +      - name: Install locked dependencies
[codex] +        run: npm ci
[codex] +
[codex] +      - name: Capture full and production audits
[codex] +        shell: bash
[codex] +        run: |
[codex] +          set -u
[codex] +
[codex] +          set +e
[codex] +          npm audit --json \
[codex] +            > dependency-audit-reports/npm-audit-full.json \
[codex] +            2> dependency-audit-reports/npm-audit-full.stderr.log
[codex] +          full_status=$?
[codex] +          npm audit --omit=dev --json \
[codex] +            > dependency-audit-reports/npm-audit-production.json \
[codex] +            2> dependency-audit-reports/npm-audit-production.stderr.log
[codex] +          production_status=$?
[codex] +          set -e
[codex] +
[codex] +          audit_operational_failure=0
[codex] +
[codex] +          classify_audit() {
[codex] +            scope="$1"
[codex] +            report="$2"
[codex] +            command_status="$3"
[codex] +
[codex] +            if [[ ! -s "${report}" ]]; then
[codex] +              echo "${scope} npm audit produced no JSON report." >&2
[codex] +              audit_operational_failure=1
[codex] +              return
[codex] +            fi
[codex] +
[codex] +            if ! jq -e 'type == "object"' "${report}" > /dev/null; then
[codex] +              echo "${scope} npm audit produced invalid JSON." >&2
[codex] +              audit_operational_failure=1
[codex] +              return
[codex] +            fi
[codex] +
[codex] +            if jq -e '.error != null' "${report}" > /dev/null; then
[codex] +              jq -r '"npm audit error: \(.error.code // "unknown"): \(.error.summary // .error.message // "no message")"' "${report}" >&2
[codex] +              audit_operational_failure=1
[codex] +              return
[codex] +            fi
[codex] +
[codex] +            if ! jq -e '.metadata.vulnerabilities.total | numbers' "${report}" > /dev/null; then
[codex] +              echo "${scope} npm audit JSON has no vulnerability metadata." >&2
[codex] +              audit_operational_failure=1
[codex] +              return
[codex] +            fi
[codex] +
[codex] +            total="$(jq -r '.metadata.vulnerabilities.total' "${report}")"
[codex] +            if [[ "${command_status}" -eq 0 && "${total}" -ne 0 ]] \
[codex] +              || [[ "${command_status}" -eq 1 && "${total}" -eq 0 ]] \
[codex] +              || [[ "${command_status}" -ne 0 && "${command_status}" -ne 1 ]]; then
[codex] +              echo "${scope} npm audit returned unexpected status ${command_status} for ${total} findings." >&2
[codex] +              audit_operational_failure=1
[codex] +              return
[codex] +            fi
[codex] +
[codex] +            jq -r --arg scope "${scope}" '
[codex] +              .metadata.vulnerabilities |
[codex] +              "| \($scope) | \(.critical) | \(.high) | \(.moderate) | \(.low) | \(.total) |"
[codex] +            ' "${report}" >> "${GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY}"
[codex] +          }
[codex] +
[codex] +          {
[codex] +            echo '## npm audit results'
[codex] +            echo
[codex] +            echo '| Scope | Critical | High | Moderate | Low | Total |'
[codex] +            echo '| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |'
[codex] +          } >> "${GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY}"
[codex] +
[codex] +          classify_audit \
[codex] +            "Full dependency tree" \
[codex] +            dependency-audit-reports/npm-audit-full.json \
[codex] +            "${full_status}"
[codex] +          classify_audit \
[codex] +            "Production dependencies" \
[codex] +            dependency-audit-reports/npm-audit-production.json \
[codex] +            "${production_status}"
[codex] +
[codex] +          {
[codex] +            printf 'full_status=%s\n' "${full_status}"
[codex] +            printf 'production_status=%s\n' "${production_status}"
[codex] +            printf 'operational_failure=%s\n' "${audit_operational_failure}"
[codex] +          } > dependency-audit-reports/status.txt
[codex] +
[codex] +          if [[ "${audit_operational_failure}" -ne 0 ]]; then
[codex] +            exit 1
[codex] +          fi
[codex] +
[codex] +          if [[ "${full_status}" -eq 1 || "${production_status}" -eq 1 ]]; then
[codex] +            echo 'Vulnerability findings were retained as evidence and do not fail this scheduled report.' >> "${GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY}"
[codex] +          fi
[codex] +
[codex] +      - name: Upload audit reports
[codex] +        if: ${{ always() }}
[codex] +        uses: actions/upload-artifact@v7
[codex] +        with:
[codex] +          name: npm-audit-reports
[codex] +          path: dependency-audit-reports/
[codex] +          if-no-files-found: warn
[codex] +          retention-days: 7

[codex] @@ -0,0 +1,147 @@
[codex] +# Dependency Automation
[codex] +
[codex] +**Status:** Local configuration and audit workflow prepared; automation is not
[codex] +installed or active. Authenticated acceptance is pending the system-wide
[codex] +activation phase.
[codex] +
[codex] +This repository uses Renovate as the sole owner of dependency-update pull
[codex] +requests. GitHub's dependency graph and Dependabot alerts provide alert-only
[codex] +vulnerability evidence; Dependabot version-update and security-update pull
[codex] +requests must remain disabled. The shared operating policy is owned by the
[codex] +[orchestration dependency-automation guide](https://github.com/budgetanalyzer/orchestration/blob/main/docs/dependency-automation.md).
[codex] +
[codex] +## Update discovery
[codex] +
[codex] +`renovate.json` extends the Budget Analyzer shared preset. Renovate discovers:
[codex] +
[codex] +- direct npm dependencies and lockfile selections from `package.json` and
[codex] +  `package-lock.json`;
[codex] +- Node and NGINX build/runtime images, tags, and digests in all three
[codex] +  Dockerfiles;
[codex] +- pinned GitHub Actions and `actions/setup-node` runtime inputs in the workflow
[codex] +  files.
[codex] +
[codex] +Routine updates and lockfile maintenance run on the shared weekly schedule.
[codex] +React and React DOM updates are grouped, as are the Vitest runner, UI, and
[codex] +coverage packages. The shared policy disables automerge, keeps patch, minor,
[codex] +and major proposals distinguishable, and requires Dependency Dashboard approval
[codex] +for majors. Toolchain majors such as TypeScript, Vite, Vitest, ESLint, Tailwind,
[codex] +React Router, and Node therefore remain visible for compatibility review rather
[codex] +than being excluded or constrained to historical target versions.
[codex] +
[codex] +Lockfile maintenance is discovery work, not permission to run `npm audit fix` or
[codex] +silently refresh the checked-in lockfile during onboarding. Review dependency
[codex] +and lockfile diffs together. For image updates, retain immutable digests and
[codex] +verify the selected tag/digest supports the required platform before merging.
[codex] +
[codex] +## Audit evidence
[codex] +
[codex] +`.github/workflows/dependency-audit.yml` runs weekly and on manual dispatch. It
[codex] +installs exactly `package-lock.json` with `npm ci`, then independently captures:
[codex] +
[codex] +- `npm audit --json` as `npm-audit-full.json`;
[codex] +- `npm audit --omit=dev --json` as `npm-audit-production.json`.
[codex] +
[codex] +Download the `npm-audit-reports` artifact from the workflow run to compare the
[codex] +raw JSON reports. The artifact is retained for seven days and also includes each
[codex] +command's stderr and `status.txt`. The job summary presents severity counts for
[codex] +the two scopes.
[codex] +
[codex] +An npm audit exit status caused by findings does not fail this scheduled report;
[codex] +the existing backlog remains visible without blocking unrelated pull requests.
[codex] +Missing or malformed JSON, an npm-reported registry error, an unexpected command
[codex] +status, or an `npm ci` failure does fail the workflow. Installation errors remain
[codex] +separate in the named install step's log, while audit errors and partial report
[codex] +files remain available through the always-upload artifact step.
[codex] +
[codex] +The credential-free onboarding run on 2026-09-06 reproduced the saved review
[codex] +against the unchanged lockfile:
[codex] +
[codex] +| Scope                   | Critical | High | Moderate | Low | Total | Top-level vulnerable paths                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
[codex] +| ----------------------- | -------: | ---: | -------: | --: | ----: | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
[codex] +| Full dependency tree    |        3 |   13 |        2 |   2 |    20 | `@babel/core`, `@humanfs/node`, `@vitest/coverage-v8`, `@vitest/ui`, `axios`, `brace-expansion`, `browserslist`, `fflate`, `flatted`, `form-data`, `js-yaml`, `nanoid`, `picomatch`, `postcss`, `postcss-selector-parser`, `react-router`, `react-router-dom`, `vite`, `vitest`, `ws` |
[codex] +| Production dependencies |        0 |    4 |        0 |   0 |     4 | `axios`, `form-data`, `react-router`, `react-router-dom`                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
[codex] +
[codex] +Both commands returned status 1 because findings exist, produced valid
[codex] +vulnerability metadata, and reported no registry error. Compare advisory and
[codex] +package identities in future runs rather than relying on the historical total:
[codex] +new advisories can appear while old ones are remediated.
[codex] +
[codex] +Reachability and runtime applicability remain human triage. In particular,
[codex] +development-server findings are not production-browser findings, and this SPA
[codex] +does not use React Server Components or React Router's server/framework modes.
[codex] +Those distinctions inform priority but do not justify dropping findings from the
[codex] +full report.
[codex] +
[codex] +## Credential-free validation evidence
[codex] +
[codex] +The onboarding extraction ran under Node 24 with Renovate 44.65.5 and found 65
[codex] +dependency occurrences across seven files: 44 npm declarations, five Dockerfile
[codex] +declarations across all three Dockerfiles, and 16 GitHub Actions declarations
[codex] +across all three workflows. The Dockerfile set included the three Node build
[codex] +images, the NGINX runtime image, and the Dockerfile syntax image. The Actions set
[codex] +included pinned action versions and both `actions/setup-node` Node 22 inputs.
[codex] +
[codex] +Public npm lookups produced current-line and later-major proposals, including
[codex] +the saved review's React, React Router, Axios, Vite, Vitest, and PostCSS package
[codex] +identities. Later TypeScript, Vite, Vitest, ESLint, Tailwind, and React Router
[codex] +lines remained visible. React/React DOM and all three direct Vitest packages
[codex] +resolved onto their respective grouped branches, and the enabled lockfile
[codex] +maintenance configuration produced a distinct maintenance branch.
[codex] +
[codex] +GitHub Actions and setup-node lookups reported `github-token-required`; those
[codex] +authenticated lookups are deliberately pending the activation phase. The local
[codex] +Docker Hub lookup did not authenticate and remains an unresolved environment
[codex] +limitation: Renovate repeatedly emitted
[codex] +`ERR_SOCKET_CLOSED_BEFORE_CONNECTION` while obtaining anonymous Docker tokens
[codex] +and recorded `no-result` for `node` and `nginxinc/nginx-unprivileged`. Direct
[codex] +anonymous checks of Docker's token endpoint succeeded, so this is not recorded
[codex] +as an authentication deferral or a successful image lookup. No runtime-image
[codex] +proposal from the local run is accepted as evidence; activation must demonstrate
[codex] +the hosted lookups or stop for diagnosis.
[codex] +
[codex] +## Bot pull request checks
[codex] +
[codex] +Every Renovate pull request remains non-automerged. The existing `Build`
[codex] +pull-request workflow runs locked installation, ESLint, the Vitest coverage gate,
[codex] +the production-smoke CSP gate, and the standard TypeScript/Vite bundle. Reviewers
[codex] +must also inspect release notes, the resolved lockfile change, browser and SSR/RSC
[codex] +applicability, strict-CSP behavior, and Node/image platform compatibility.
[codex] +
[codex] +The [development guide](development.md#repository-commands) owns local build and
[codex] +validation commands. CSP-sensitive dependency changes must also follow the
[codex] +[architecture CSP contract](architecture.md#content-security-policy) and the
[codex] +[external browser harness](testing-guide.md#external-browser-harness); the bot
[codex] +cannot infer runtime stylesheet injection or mobile/browser compatibility from
[codex] +a successful package lookup.
[codex] +
[codex] +## Phase 12 hosted and authenticated evidence handoff
[codex] +
[codex] +No GitHub, registry, or Mend credentials are required or used during local
[codex] +preparation. The activation operator must complete and retain these checks:
[codex] +
[codex] +1. Publish the orchestration preset, install the free Mend Renovate Community
[codex] +   App for this repository, and retain the hosted Renovate log or Dependency
[codex] +   Dashboard URL proving the preset resolves and npm, lockfile-maintenance,
[codex] +   Dockerfile, GitHub Actions, and setup-node inputs are discovered without
[codex] +   authentication, configuration, timeout, or lookup failures. In particular,
[codex] +   prove successful lookups for `node` and `nginxinc/nginx-unprivileged`; if the
[codex] +   local anonymous-token socket failure repeats in the hosted run, stop
[codex] +   activation and diagnose it rather than treating extraction alone as update
[codex] +   coverage.
[codex] +2. Dispatch or observe `.github/workflows/dependency-audit.yml` on trusted
[codex] +   `main`. Retain the successful workflow URL and `npm-audit-reports` artifact
[codex] +   proving `npm ci` succeeded and both raw JSON scopes were captured. A findings
[codex] +   exit status is expected; installation, registry, and malformed-report errors
[codex] +   must still fail the job.
[codex] +3. Confirm the dependency graph and Dependabot alerts are enabled while
[codex] +   Dependabot update pull requests remain disabled. Retain alert URLs and map
[codex] +   advisory/package identities to the two audit reports, including explained
[codex] +   misses and production-versus-development applicability.
[codex] +4. Retain the successful existing `Build` workflow URL for representative bot
[codex] +   pull requests, including lockfile maintenance and a toolchain-major proposal
[codex] +   after dashboard approval.
[codex] +
[codex] +The orchestration coverage report owns final cross-repository acceptance. Local
[codex] +preparation does not activate Renovate, prove hosted audit execution, or establish
[codex] +vulnerability-review parity.

[codex] +name: Dependency Audit
[codex] +
[codex] +on:
[codex] +  schedule:
[codex] +    - cron: '17 6 * * 1'
[codex] +  workflow_dispatch:
[codex] +
[codex] +permissions:
[codex] +  contents: read
[codex] +
[codex] +concurrency:
[codex] +  group: dependency-audit
[codex] +  cancel-in-progress: false
[codex] +
[codex] +env:
[codex] +  FORCE_JAVASCRIPT_ACTIONS_TO_NODE24: 'true'
[codex] +
[codex] +jobs:
[codex] +  npm-audit:
[codex] +    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
[codex] +
[codex] +    steps:
[codex] +      - name: Checkout code
[codex] +        uses: actions/checkout@v6
[codex] +
[codex] +      - name: Set up Node.js
[codex] +        uses: actions/setup-node@v6
[codex] +        with:
[codex] +          node-version: '22'
[codex] +          cache: 'npm'
[codex] +
[codex] +      - name: Prepare report directory
[codex] +        run: mkdir -p dependency-audit-reports
[codex] +
[codex] +      - name: Install locked dependencies
[codex] +        run: npm ci
[codex] +
[codex] +      - name: Capture full and production audits
[codex] +        shell: bash
[codex] +        run: |
[codex] +          set -u
[codex] +
[codex] +          set +e
[codex] +          npm audit --json \
[codex] +            > dependency-audit-reports/npm-audit-full.json \
[codex] +            2> dependency-audit-reports/npm-audit-full.stderr.log
[codex] +          full_status=$?
[codex] +          npm audit --omit=dev --json \
[codex] +            > dependency-audit-reports/npm-audit-production.json \
[codex] +            2> dependency-audit-reports/npm-audit-production.stderr.log
[codex] +          production_status=$?
[codex] +          set -e
[codex] +
[codex] +          audit_operational_failure=0
[codex] +
[codex] +          classify_audit() {
[codex] +            scope="$1"
[codex] +            report="$2"
[codex] +            command_status="$3"
[codex] +
[codex] +            if [[ ! -s "${report}" ]]; then
[codex] +              echo "${scope} npm audit produced no JSON report." >&2
[codex] +              audit_operational_failure=1
[codex] +              return
[codex] +            fi
[codex] +
[codex] +            if ! jq -e 'type == "object"' "${report}" > /dev/null; then
[codex] +              echo "${scope} npm audit produced invalid JSON." >&2
[codex] +              audit_operational_failure=1
[codex] +              return
[codex] +            fi
[codex] +
[codex] +            if jq -e '.error != null' "${report}" > /dev/null; then
[codex] +              jq -r '"npm audit error: \(.error.code // "unknown"): \(.error.summary // .error.message // "no message")"' "${report}" >&2
[codex] +              audit_operational_failure=1
[codex] +              return
[codex] +            fi
[codex] +
[codex] +            if ! jq -e '.metadata.vulnerabilities.total | numbers' "${report}" > /dev/null; then
[codex] +              echo "${scope} npm audit JSON has no vulnerability metadata." >&2
[codex] +              audit_operational_failure=1
[codex] +              return
[codex] +            fi
[codex] +
[codex] +            total="$(jq -r '.metadata.vulnerabilities.total' "${report}")"
[codex] +            if [[ "${command_status}" -eq 0 && "${total}" -ne 0 ]] \
[codex] +              || [[ "${command_status}" -eq 1 && "${total}" -eq 0 ]] \
[codex] +              || [[ "${command_status}" -ne 0 && "${command_status}" -ne 1 ]]; then
[codex] +              echo "${scope} npm audit returned unexpected status ${command_status} for ${total} findings." >&2
[codex] +              audit_operational_failure=1
[codex] +              return
[codex] +            fi
[codex] +
[codex] +            jq -r --arg scope "${scope}" '
[codex] +              .metadata.vulnerabilities |
[codex] +              "| \($scope) | \(.critical) | \(.high) | \(.moderate) | \(.low) | \(.total) |"
[codex] +            ' "${report}" >> "${GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY}"
[codex] +          }
[codex] +
[codex] +          {
[codex] +            echo '## npm audit results'
[codex] +            echo
[codex] +            echo '| Scope | Critical | High | Moderate | Low | Total |'
[codex] +            echo '| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |'
[codex] +          } >> "${GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY}"
[codex] +
[codex] +          classify_audit \
[codex] +            "Full dependency tree" \
[codex] +            dependency-audit-reports/npm-audit-full.json \
[codex] +            "${full_status}"
[codex] +          classify_audit \
[codex] +            "Production dependencies" \
[codex] +            dependency-audit-reports/npm-audit-production.json \
[codex] +            "${production_status}"
[codex] +
[codex] +          {
[codex] +            printf 'full_status=%s\n' "${full_status}"
[codex] +            printf 'production_status=%s\n' "${production_status}"
[codex] +            printf 'operational_failure=%s\n' "${audit_operational_failure}"
[codex] +          } > dependency-audit-reports/status.txt
[codex] +
[codex] +          if [[ "${audit_operational_failure}" -ne 0 ]]; then
[codex] +            exit 1
[codex] +          fi
[codex] +
[codex] +          if [[ "${full_status}" -eq 1 || "${production_status}" -eq 1 ]]; then
[codex] +            echo 'Vulnerability findings were retained as evidence and do not fail this scheduled report.' >> "${GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY}"
[codex] +          fi
[codex] +
[codex] +      - name: Upload audit reports
[codex] +        if: ${{ always() }}
[codex] +        uses: actions/upload-artifact@v7
[codex] +        with:
[codex] +          name: npm-audit-reports
[codex] +          path: dependency-audit-reports/
[codex] +          if-no-files-found: warn
[codex] +          retention-days: 7

[codex]

[codex]  - UI dependency, Motion, overlay, or CSP-sensitive changes: also run the static

[codex] +- Dependency-automation configuration or workflow changes: format changed
